# Content critique — 30-chapter audit (2026-07-13)

> Evidence base for the improvement plan (`~/.claude/plans/this-is-an-llm-snoopy-zebra.md`).
> Produced by a 37-agent read-only critique: one deep reviewer per chapter (every
> runnable block executed, every cross-reference resolved against the canonical map),
> 6 cross-cutting sweeps, and a synthesis. Findings marked `PLAUSIBLE` need confirmation
> (especially runnable/widget claims — verify in the real Pyodide runtime); `CONFIRMED`
> were substantiated against the text or the reviewer's knowledge.

## Overall verdict

This is a strong, unusually careful textbook whose core mathematical derivations are largely correct and whose prose voice is disciplined — the median chapter earns a solid B and several (Ch5 A-, Ch1/Ch2/Ch12/Ch13/Ch26 B+) are genuinely good. But it is not yet 'truly high quality' because its defects cluster in exactly the surfaces a learner touches most: the interactive runnables and widgets. Across ~14 chapters a flagship demo or widget prints output that contradicts, and sometimes outright refutes, the lesson it exists to teach (Ch17's central KV-cache demo crashes; Ch8/Ch9's optimizer and scaling widgets demonstrate the opposite of their thesis; Ch11's param-count code contradicts numbers repeated ten times; Ch22's two headline demos can't even load). Layered on top are pervasive coverage gaps against the curriculum's own spec (whole objectives like Ch14 Constitutional AI and Ch29 MCP/A2A simply absent), a recurring class of exercises whose correct solution disagrees with the stated answer, a scattering of confidently-wrong model facts and attributions, and back-third voice drift. None of these are deep — they are almost all mechanically findable and fixable. Reaching 'truly high quality' requires two systematic sweeps above all: (1) execute every runnable in the real Pyodide runtime and diff its output against its own narration, treating every mismatch as build-blocking; and (2) diff each chapter against its CURRICULUM spec to close the named-objective and named-concept gaps. Do those two plus the fact-check and exercise-reconciliation passes, and this becomes an excellent book; the skeleton already deserves it.

## Cross-cutting themes

### 1. [CRITICAL] Runnable code and widgets whose printed output or rendered data contradicts (often refutes) the lesson they exist to teach
- **Evidence:** ~14 chapters. Blocker/major code-or-widget-vs-lesson defects in Ch7 (quality filter fails every sample), Ch8 (OptimizerComparison shows SGD winning; CE demo contradicts its output), Ch9 (Chinchilla D/N prints 320-696 vs claimed ~20, across code+widget+exercise), Ch11 (2-matrix FFN yields 32B vs 46.7B stated ~10x), Ch13 (synthetic filter keeps bad/drops good), Ch16 (T=1 0.83 vs 0.55), Ch17 (KV-cache demo crashes), Ch18 (NF4 levels), Ch20/27/29 (widget curves/arithmetic), Ch22 (both flagship demos can't load in Pyodide), Ch23 ('car' matches 'care'), Ch24 (two demos), Ch25 (SAE recovers 122 not 12; Ex3 100% everywhere).
- **Recommendation:** Systematic pass: build a harness that executes every RunnableCode block in Pyodide (not just python3) and diffs actual stdout against the block's inline '# Observations' comments; flag every mismatch. Separately recompute every widget's hardcoded numbers/annotations against its own data file. Treat any demo that contradicts its narration as a build-blocking defect.

### 2. [CRITICAL] Missing curriculum-specced content — named concepts, papers, objectives, and required runnables silently dropped
- **Evidence:** Nearly every chapter. Full learning objectives absent: Ch14 Constitutional AI/RLAIF (titled topic), Ch29 MCP/A2A protocols, Ch10 compiler-stacks + observability/fault-tolerance, Ch30 agent-framework comparison. Specced key concepts/papers missing: Ch1 death-of-ReLU, Ch3 decode/round-trip, Ch7 RefinedWeb, Ch8 TinyStories, Ch13 FLAN, Ch15 VeRA+PyTorch-LoRA, Ch16 reverse-KL/on-vs-off-policy, Ch18 GGUF+KV-cache-quant, Ch19 min-p/top-k/contrastive, Ch20 GRPO-mechanics/aha-moment, Ch21 tau-bench, Ch22 RAG-vs-long-context/MMR/ColBERT, Ch23 VQ-VAE/video, Ch24 many-shot-jailbreak, Ch26 pass@k/Elo/tau-bench, Ch28 state-machine/Pydantic-schema-gen, Ch30 Upwork-HAPI.
- **Recommendation:** Mechanical gap-closing pass: for each chapter, diff CURRICULUM.md 'Reader will be able to' + 'Key concepts' + 'Runnable code' lists against the shipped chapter (grep for each named term/paper). Produce a per-chapter missing-items checklist and add the absent sections/runnables. Prioritize items that are named learning objectives or titled topics over passing key-concept mentions.

### 3. [HIGH] Wrong technical/factual claims stated with confidence — model facts, attributions, and math
- **Evidence:** Many chapters. Model facts: Ch1/Ch2 (Llama/Mistral bias & weight-tying), Ch3 (Mistral tokenizer), Ch10 (Llama-3 FP8 training, 16M vs 31M GPU-hours), Ch13 (begin_of_text=BOS). Attributions: Ch3 (WordPiece=Schuster&Nakajima not Wu), Ch6 (NTK scaling), Ch20 (PRM800K human-labeled; R1 vs R1-Zero; Snell finding inverted), Ch25 (TransformerLens not EleutherAI), Ch29 (likely-fabricated Hoff&Anderson 1976; Du 2023 debate not proposer-critic), Ch26/30 (Chen vs Chan; GPT-4 mini). Math: Ch4/Ch9/Ch10/Ch12/Ch17/Ch18 memory/FLOP numbers off by 2-16x.
- **Recommendation:** Fact-check pass focused on three buckets: (1) which production models use which technique (biases, weight-tying, tokenizers, FP8) — verify against reference configs; (2) paper first-author/year attributions — verify against arXiv; (3) all memory/FLOP arithmetic — recompute with dtype made explicit. Confirm or delete the Hoff&Anderson 1976 citation specifically.

### 4. [MEDIUM] Currency / staleness — hardware and model rosters pinned to early-2025 for a mid-2026 reader
- **Evidence:** Book already stamps most claims 'as of early 2025', but Ch10 hardware roster stops at Hopper/MI300X with zero Blackwell/H200 mention while using unqualified present tense ('the current workhorse'). Secondary aging: Ch4 (80GB GPU ceiling), Ch11 (DeepSeek-V3 as open SOTA), Ch20 (o1/R1 as default), Ch23 (Claude voice 'on roadmap', shipped VLM versions), Ch27/29 (Assistants API, OpenAI Swarm superseded), Ch30 (GPT-4o/mini tier).
- **Recommendation:** Adopt one house convention repo-wide: every leaderboard/hardware/'state-of-the-art' claim gets an explicit 'as of <date>' stamp plus a 'this will move' caveat (most chapters already do this — extend to Ch10's accelerator/pricing paragraphs). Prefer model-family/tier language over point-version names for live-capability claims. Add one Blackwell/H200 entry to Ch10.

### 5. [MEDIUM] Two parallel section-numbering systems + stale chapter cross-references
- **Evidence:** Cross-cutting sweep: reader-facing site says 'Part I-IX' but chapter prose Ch10-30 uses undefined 'Phase 9-15' (169 occurrences, Phase N = Part N-6), appearing abruptly at Ch10. Plus Ch4 has 4 stale chapter refs (block='Ch7' should be Ch5; SSM='Ch11' should be Ch12), 1 dangling cross-chapter EqRef in Ch13 (#eq-8.loss), 1 off-by-one section ref in Ch19 (Ch18 §6 should be §7), 1 conflation in Ch30 (block=Ch4 should be Ch5).
- **Recommendation:** Two mechanical passes: (1) global find/replace 'Phase N' -> matching 'Part <roman>'/part-name throughout Ch10-30 so narration matches the nav; (2) fix the enumerated stale refs in Ch4/Ch13/Ch19/Ch30. Add a CI check that greps for 'Phase [0-9]+' and for chapter-number claims that disagree with the canonical map.

### 6. [HIGH] Exercises that mislead — wrong expected values, dead code branches, or degenerate demonstrations
- **Evidence:** ~12 chapters. A correct student solution disagrees with the exercise: Ch8 Ex2 (expects 2.5-2.9, converges 2.998), Ch9 Ex2 (expects r=20, gets 61-123), Ch11 Ex2 (verify-target unreachable + backwards memory claim), Ch15 Ex2/Ex3 (backwards observation; transposed merge fails equivalence), Ch17 Ex1 (128K-labeled values are 8K values), Ch22 Ex4 + Ch30 Ex3 (degenerate: 100% recall everywhere / impossible domination), Ch25 Ex3 (patching 100% at every layer), Ch27/28/29 (dead weather/author branches from proxy-guards keyed on the wrong string).
- **Recommendation:** Run every exercise's own reference/intended solution and compare against its stated expected-output comments; fix the mismatches. Establish a pattern rule against mock-LLM guards that key on substrings present in the task string (the recurring 'Tokyo not in prompt' / 'car in caring' class of bug). Verify each exercise can actually demonstrate the behavior it claims.

### 7. [MEDIUM] Voice-contract drift concentrated in the back third — announce-the-next-section transitions, bold-fragment escalation, leaked authoring notes
- **Evidence:** Voice sweep: ~128 'The next section covers X' transitions across ~25 chapters (explicitly forbidden pattern). Bold-span density climbs monotonically (Ch1-5: 0-9; Ch27-30: 167-187, with 58-73 whole-sentence bolds). Curriculum-countdown fragments ('Four chapters from the end.', 'Now let's finish.') in Ch23/25-30. Blocker-level leaked authoring-spec prose in Ch28/Ch30 intros ('The tonal frame is engineering with empathy for the LLM'; 'The closing thought sends the reader off well'). A few 'How X works' gerund headings in Ch9/Ch13.
- **Recommendation:** Automated + manual pass: grep-delete bare 'The next section...' sentences and countdown fragments; demote whole-sentence bolds in Ch21-30 to plain prose (target front-chapter density); delete leaked authoring notes in Ch28/Ch30 intros; rename 'How X works' headings to name the mechanism. Add a lint rule for the forbidden-phrase list.

## Prioritized workstreams

### [P0] Execute-and-diff every RunnableCode block against its own claimed output, in the real Pyodide runtime  _(effort: Large (harness + ~25-30 individual fixes))_
- **Scope:** All 30 chapters; acute in Ch7, Ch8, Ch9, Ch11, Ch13, Ch16, Ch17, Ch18, Ch22, Ch23, Ch24, Ch25
- **Approach:** Build a script that extracts every RunnableCode `defaultCode` block, runs it under Pyodide (matching the site runtime, not local numpy — this catches the Ch22 rank_bm25/sentence_transformers non-load and the macOS-BLAS false positives), and diffs stdout against the block's inline '# Observations'/comment claims. For each mismatch, fix the code so output matches the narration (or vice versa). Explicit fixes already localized: Ch17 KV-cache demo shape errors (both paths), Ch11 FFN 2->3 matrix, Ch9 Chinchilla D/N (recompute constants or use the r-sweep), Ch7 quality-filter length threshold, Ch8 CE demo logit variance + OptimizerComparison LR calibration, Ch13/Ch24 filter/observation blocks, Ch16/Ch18 hardcoded level/prob values, Ch23 'car'-in-'care' whole-word matching, Ch25 SAE decoder-norm constraint. For Ch22, convert library-dependent demos to precomputed-embedding + pure-numpy per the spec.

### [P0] Curriculum coverage-gap closure: diff every chapter against its CURRICULUM spec and add missing objectives/concepts/runnables  _(effort: Large)_
- **Scope:** All 30 chapters; full-objective gaps in Ch14 (CAI/RLAIF), Ch29 (MCP/A2A), Ch10 (compiler-stacks + observability), Ch30 (agent frameworks)
- **Approach:** For each chapter, mechanically grep the shipped MDX for every term/paper named in CURRICULUM.md's 'Reader will be able to', 'Key concepts', and 'Runnable code' lists; emit a per-chapter checklist of absent items. Then author the missing sections, prioritizing named objectives and titled topics (Ch14 CAI is in the chapter title) over passing mentions. Batch the smaller additions (Ch1 dead-ReLU, Ch3 decode, Ch7 RefinedWeb, Ch8 TinyStories, Ch13 FLAN, Ch15 VeRA, Ch18 GGUF/KV-quant, Ch19 min-p/top-k, Ch22 MMR/long-context, Ch23 VQ-VAE, Ch24 many-shot, Ch26 pass@k/Elo, Ch30 Upwork-HAPI). Where CURRICULUM.md is merely stale vs the shipped widgets (renamed components), update the doc rather than the build.

### [P1] Fix chapter cross-references and unify the Part/Phase numbering system  _(effort: Medium)_
- **Scope:** Ch4 (4 stale refs), Ch13 (dangling EqRef), Ch19 (off-by-one section ref), Ch30 (block conflation), plus Ch10-30 prose (169 'Phase N')
- **Approach:** Two find/replace passes. (1) Global: replace 'Phase N' with the matching 'Part <roman>'/part-name across Ch10-30 so narration matches the reader-facing nav. (2) Targeted: Ch4 block 'Chapter 7'->'Chapter 5' (x2) and SSM 'Chapter 11'->'Chapter 12' (x2); Ch13 replace `<EqRef id="8.loss">` with a prose 'Chapter 8' reference or make EqRef emit a cross-chapter URL; Ch19 'Ch 18 §6'->'§7'; Ch30 'transformer block ... Chapter 4'->'attention operation ... Chapter 4'. Add a CI grep that flags 'Phase [0-9]+' and chapter-number claims against a canonical map.

### [P1] Run and reconcile every exercise's intended solution against its stated expected output  _(effort: Medium-Large)_
- **Scope:** Ch8, Ch9, Ch11, Ch15, Ch17, Ch22, Ch25, Ch27, Ch28, Ch29, Ch30 (and Ch2 corpus wiring)
- **Approach:** For each exercise, implement/execute the reference solution the hint describes and compare to the exercise's expected-value comments; fix whichever is wrong. Specific fixes: Ch8 Ex2 expected 2.98-3.00, Ch9 Ex2 state true r=60-120 drift, Ch11 Ex2 3-matrix FFN + fix memory-footprint comment, Ch15 Ex3 merge = A.T@B.T (or make dims non-square) + Ex2 flip observation #4, Ch17 Ex1 relabel 8K vs 128K, Ch22 Ex4 + Ch30 Ex3 make mocks discriminate (recall/domination), Ch25 Ex3 give the toy model branching structure, Ch2 wire one consistent corpus through Ex1-4. Add a lint rule against mock-LLM guards that key on substrings present in the task string (Ch27/28/29 dead-branch class).

### [P1] Fact-check pass: model facts, paper attributions, and memory/FLOP arithmetic  _(effort: Medium-Large)_
- **Scope:** Ch1, Ch2, Ch3, Ch4, Ch6, Ch9, Ch10, Ch12, Ch13, Ch17, Ch18, Ch20, Ch25, Ch26, Ch29, Ch30
- **Approach:** Three checklists. (1) Production-model facts vs reference configs: Llama/Mistral biases (Ch1) & weight-tying (Ch2), Mistral tokenizer (Ch3), Llama-3 FP8-training (Ch10), begin_of_text=BOS (Ch13), R1-vs-R1-Zero SFT (Ch20). (2) Attributions vs arXiv: WordPiece->Schuster&Nakajima 2012 (Ch3), NTK-scaling->community (Ch6), PRM800K human-labeled + Snell finding (Ch20), TransformerLens->Nanda (Ch25), Du 2023 = debate (Ch29), MLE-bench->Chan (Ch26); and confirm-or-delete Hoff&Anderson 1976 (Ch29). (3) Recompute all memory/FLOP numbers with dtype made explicit: Ch4 (32GB single-matrix framing), Ch9 (12 vs 18 bytes/param), Ch10 (FlashAttention O(N^2+Nd), 40MB L2, GPU-hours), Ch12 (BF16 2GB not 4GB), Ch17 (KV-cache 8-14x errors), Ch18 (bit-overhead dtype).

### [P1] Widget data/annotation audit: recompute every hardcoded number and caption against the widget's own data file  _(effort: Medium)_
- **Scope:** Ch4 (AttentionHeatmap), Ch8 (OptimizerComparison), Ch9 (ScalingLawCalculator), Ch5/Ch12/Ch14/Ch20/Ch23/Ch24/Ch26/Ch27 captions & dead controls
- **Approach:** For each widget, load its data.ts and recompute the values the caption/insight text asserts; fix mismatches. Fix dead controls: Ch14 DPO beta slider (re-parameterize axes or remove), Ch11 MoERoutingVisualizer (add temperature/penalty sliders per spec). Fix caption counts/values: Ch5 after-LN normalization, Ch12 crossover, Ch20 asymptote-vs-endpoint, Ch23 6/6-vs-7/5, Ch24 sample counts, Ch26 SAT badge, Ch27 arithmetic. Correct BrowseComp year (Ch30).

### [P2] Voice-contract cleanup pass across the back third  _(effort: Medium)_
- **Scope:** Ch21-30 densest; 'next section' transitions in ~25 chapters; leaked authoring notes in Ch28/Ch30
- **Approach:** Mechanical + manual: (1) grep-delete the ~128 bare 'The next section covers X' sentences and the curriculum-countdown fragments ('Four chapters from the end.', 'Now let's finish.'); (2) delete leaked authoring-spec prose in Ch28/Ch30 intros; (3) demote whole-sentence bolds in Ch21-30 to plain prose, targeting the Ch1-5 density (reserve bold for term-introduction); (4) rename 'How X works' headings (Ch9/Ch13) to name the mechanism; (5) trim marketing-flavored openers (Ch21/22/28/30) to the measured 'By the end, the reader can...' form. Add the forbidden-phrase list as a lint rule.

### [P2] Currency-hardening: stamp and genericize live-capability claims, refresh Ch10 hardware  _(effort: Small-Medium)_
- **Scope:** Ch10 (hardware/pricing) primary; Ch4/Ch11/Ch20/Ch23/Ch27/Ch29/Ch30 secondary
- **Approach:** Adopt one repo-wide convention: every leaderboard/hardware/SOTA claim gets an explicit 'as of <date>' stamp + a 'this will move' caveat (most chapters already do — extend to Ch10). Add a Blackwell (B200/GB200) and H200 entry to Ch10 and reframe 'current workhorse' as '2024-era'. Replace point-version model names with family/tier language for live-capability claims (Ch11 DeepSeek-V3, Ch20 o1/R1, Ch23 VLM list + Claude voice, Ch30 GPT-4o/mini tier). Note superseded tools (Ch27 Assistants API, Ch29 OpenAI Swarm). Keep dated historical facts as-is.

### [P3] Reconcile CURRICULUM.md as an accurate inventory of what shipped  _(effort: Small)_
- **Scope:** Whole book; widget-name renames (~15 chapters), Ch28 missing state-machine widget, live-API runnable specs (Ch21/26/27/28/30), Ch9/Ch10 'None runnable' claims
- **Approach:** Update CURRICULUM.md widget one-liners to shipped component names (or accept it defers to prompts/ as source of truth). Reconcile the live-Anthropic-API runnable specs with reality: either mark them static/schematic (RunnableCode is Pyodide-only, no network) or note the JS-bridge capability was cut. Correct the 'None runnable' notes for Ch9/Ch10 which actually ship 7-8 working blocks. For Ch28, either build the specced AgentStateMachineDiagram or update the spec to the shipped AgentTraceInspector+ToolSchemaBuilder.

- Ch6: swap the inverted ALiBi decay labels (head 0 is steepest, head 3 gentlest) — a blocker fixed by exchanging two print strings.

- Ch28: delete the leaked authoring-spec sentences from the Ch28/Ch30 intros ('The tonal frame is engineering with empathy...', 'The closing thought sends the reader off well').
- Ch13: delete 'distinct from the BOS token' — begin_of_text IS Llama-3's BOS (self-contradicts the same chapter).
- Ch21: delete the garbled 'twelve years younger' ReAct clause and correct Toolformer from 'a few dozen tools' to 5.
- Ch11: change ffn_per_expert from 2*d*d_ffn to 3*d*d_ffn in three places — fixes the blocker that makes every Mixtral param count contradict the prose.
- Ch30: fix Exercise 3's domination comment (sonnet-bloated is dominated by sonnet-only-default, not hybrid-haiku-sonnet) and BrowseComp year 2024->2025; 'GPT-4 mini'->'GPT-4o mini'.
- Ch19: correct 'Ch 18 §6'->'§7' and drop the false 'same max-subtraction trick' claim (Ch18 never covers softmax stability).
- Ch3: change 'the' appears 'six times'->'five times' and reattribute WordPiece to Schuster & Nakajima 2012.
- Ch1: fix the reversed SGD ill-conditioning sentence (steep direction is y with gradient 20y, not x) and delete the false Llama-keeps-FFN-bias claim.
- Ch14: correct the intro's 'three families'->'four families' count and 'Four exercises'->'Five exercises'.

## Cross-cutting sweep summaries

### cross-reference-and-anchor-integrity
Across all 30 chapters, exactly ONE chapter has stale "Chapter N" numbering: ch04-attention (the seeded defect), containing 4 wrong cross-references — 2 cite the transformer block as "Chapter 7" (it is Chapter 5) and 2 cite state-space models / the linear-scaling alternative as "Chapter 11" (SSMs are Chapter 12; Chapter 11 is MoE). Every other "Chapter N" prose mention (~230), including all forward/back recaps and phase previews, resolves correctly against the canonical map. Structured anchors are almost entirely clean: all 45 <Equation label> definitions are unique and every <EqRef> resolves EXCEPT ch13-sft's EqRef id="8.loss", which is a cross-chapter reference that renders as a same-page anchor (#eq-8.loss) not present on the ch13 page (the equation lives in ch08). Section (§) references are correct except one off-by-one in ch19 ("Ch 18 §6" should be §7, activation quantization). One borderline conflation exists in ch30 (calls the "transformer block" a Chapter-4 artifact; the block is Chapter 5, Chapter 4 is the attention operation). The suspected Flash Attention attribution conflict (ch12→Ch10 vs ch04→Ch17) is NOT a defect: Flash Attention has dedicated sections in both ch10 and ch17. Net: 1/30 chapters with stale chapter numbering (4 instances), plus 1 dangling EqRef anchor, 1 off-by-one section anchor, 1 borderline conflation.

- **[major]** Transformer block miscited as 'Chapter 7' — the block is Chapter 5 (ch05-multihead-and-block, 'Multi-head attention & the transformer block').  
  _scope:_ ch04-attention (Chapter 4)  
  _evidence:_ src/pages/ch04-attention/index.mdx:29: "The operation lives inside the transformer block (Chapter 7), which wraps it with feedforward layers, residuals, and normalization. Multi-head attention (Chapter 5) runs several copies of it in parallel." (Same sentence correctly puts multi-head in Ch5; the block is also Ch5.)  
  _fix:_ Change '(Chapter 7)' to '(Chapter 5)'.

  _scope:_ ch04-attention (Chapter 4)  
  _evidence:_ src/pages/ch04-attention/index.mdx:558: "Chapter 5 stacks multiple parallel attentions into multi-head attention. Chapter 6 adds positional information... Chapter 7 wraps attention in the transformer block."  
  _fix:_ Change 'Chapter 7 wraps attention in the transformer block' to 'Chapter 5' (Ch5 already appears earlier in the sentence; fold in).
- **[major]** State-space models miscited as 'Chapter 11' — SSMs/Mamba are Chapter 12 (ch12-ssm-and-mamba); Chapter 11 is Mixture of Experts.  
  _scope:_ ch04-attention (Chapter 4)  
  _evidence:_ src/pages/ch04-attention/index.mdx:377: "Chapter 11 introduces state-space models, alternative architectures with $O(n)$ scaling that may or may not displace attention for long-context tasks."  
  _fix:_ Change 'Chapter 11 introduces state-space models' to 'Chapter 12'.
- **[major]** The linear-scaling architectural alternative (SSMs) miscited as 'Chapter 11' in the closing summary — should be Chapter 12.  
  _scope:_ ch04-attention (Chapter 4)  
  _evidence:_ src/pages/ch04-attention/index.mdx:558: "Chapter 11 introduces an alternative that trades the formula for linear scaling." (SSM/Mamba = Ch12; ch12 confirms at ch12-ssm-and-mamba/index.mdx:430: 'Chapter 11 (Mixture of Experts), this chapter completes the architectural-alternatives arc'.)  
  _fix:_ Change 'Chapter 11 introduces an alternative that trades the formula for linear scaling' to 'Chapter 12'.
- **[major]** Dangling cross-chapter equation anchor: EqRef id="8.loss" renders as href="#eq-8.loss" (EqRef.astro is same-page only), but no <Equation label="8.loss"> exists on the ch13 page — that equation is defined in ch08 (ch08-building-small-llm/index.mdx:49). The link resolves to nothing on the ch13 page.

  _evidence:_ src/pages/ch13-sft/index.mdx:79: "...the standard per-position cross-entropy from <EqRef id=\"8.loss\" />, and the sum runs over all positions...". ch13's only local Equation label is 13.sft. EqRef.astro:7 emits <a href={`#eq-${id}`}>; Equation.astro:6 sets id only on the declaring page. Intent is correct (it IS ch08's loss, and the next sentence says 'Chapter 8's training loss'), but the anchor cannot cross chapters.

- **[minor]** Section reference off by one: 'Ch 18 §6' is labeled 'for activation quantization', but §6 of ch18 is 'Modern PTQ: GPTQ and AWQ'. Activation quantization is §7.

Convention §N=Nth ## confirmed against Ch17 §2='Prefill vs decode', Ch20 §4='ReAct', Ch19 §8='Constrained decoding', Ch27 §5='Memory and state'.

- **[minor]** Conflation: the closing calls the 'transformer block' a Chapter 4 artifact, but the transformer block is Chapter 5. Chapter 4 covers the attention operation (matrix-multiplication-and-softmax), not the block.

  _fix:_ Either change 'transformer block' to 'attention operation' (keep Chapter 4), or change 'Chapter 4' to 'Chapter 5' (keep 'transformer block'). The matmul+softmax phrasing favors 'attention operation ... Chapter 4'.

### currency
The textbook already practices strong currency discipline: most frontier claims across chapters 14, 20, 22, 23, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29, and 30 are explicitly stamped ("as of early 2025", "as of late 2024") and carry self-aware caveats that lists will date, and ch17 even uses a forward-looking range ("2024-2026"). The clearest genuine gap is ch10's hardware roster, which stops at Hopper/GH200/MI300X and never mentions NVIDIA Blackwell (B200/GB200) while calling H100 "the current workhorse" in unqualified present tense; this is the one section that reads a full GPU generation stale. Secondary, lower-severity aging shows up in named model versions positioned as current: o1/R1 as "the default" in ch20 (o3-generation successors now exist), the Claude 3.5/4 and Gemini 1.5/2.0 VLM list plus "Claude voice on the roadmap" in ch23, DeepSeek-V3 as open-weights SOTA in ch11, and the GPT-4o/GPT-4-mini frontier tier in ch30. Because the book hedges these with date stamps they age gracefully but read dated. Recommended currency-hardening strategy is uniform: extend the existing "as of <date>" + "this will move" convention to the two places that lack it (ch10 hardware and pricing), and prefer model-family/tier language over point-version names wherever a claim is about live capability rather than dated historical fact. Per instructions, findings resting on newer-model existence are kept conservative and modest-severity even where I am confident the successors (Blackwell, o3, GPT-5, Claude 4, Gemini 2.5) predate the knowledge cutoff.

- **[major]** The GPU roster stops at Hopper/GH200/MI300X and never mentions NVIDIA Blackwell (B200/GB200), the generation that shipped and became the frontier training substrate through 2025. H100 is called 'the current workhorse' and GH200 'Newer frontier clusters increasingly use GH200' in present tense. As of mid-2026 a reader sees a roster one full GPU generation behind, with no successor named.  
  _scope:_ ch10-training-infra (accelerator section); echoes into ch09, ch15  
  _evidence:_ ch10 L37 'Frontier LLM training in 2024 runs on ... a small handful of accelerators'; L39 'The NVIDIA H100 ... is the current workhorse'; L43 'GH200 ... Newer frontier clusters increasingly use GH200'; L45 MI300X 'the most credible alternative'. grep for 'Blackwell|B200|GB200|Rubin' across all 30 chapters returns zero hits.  
  _fix:_ Add one Blackwell entry (B200/GB200, ~192GB HBM3e) and re-frame the section as an explicit 2024 snapshot: change 'the current workhorse' to 'the 2024-era workhorse' and add 'By 2025 Blackwell (GB200) had become the new frontier substrate; H100 is now the prior generation, as A100 was to it.' This future-proofs the whole comparison.
- **[minor]** o1 and R1 are positioned as 'now the default' and 'state-of-the-art for hard tasks.' The era is dated (o1 Sept 2024, R1 Jan 2025, Gemini Thinking Dec 2024), which ages the history gracefully, but the forward-looking 'now the default / state of the art' framing predates the o3 / GPT-5 / Gemini 2.5 / Claude-4-extended-thinking wave that is now the actual frontier.  
  _scope:_ ch20-reasoning (sections 7-8, summary)  
  _evidence:_ ch20 L289 'Test-time compute is now a deployment knob ... o1's API exposes reasoning_effort'; L686 'modern reasoning models (o1, R1) are now the default'; L697 'modern reasoning models are state-of-the-art for hard tasks.' No successor generation named.  
  _fix:_ Keep the dated o1/R1 history; append a forward hedge: 'o1 and R1 were the first generation; successors (OpenAI's o3-series, Gemini 2.5, and others) have pushed the paradigm further, but the mechanism, RLVR-trained internal reasoning with a test-time-compute knob, is unchanged.' Soften 'now the default' to 'established the paradigm now standard.'
- **[minor]** Concrete model-version lists are aging: 'Claude 3.5 / 4 with vision', 'Gemini 1.5 / 2.0', and 'Claude voice (Anthropic, on the roadmap).' By mid-2026 Claude 4 and Gemini 2.5 are the shipped current versions and Claude voice is no longer merely 'on the roadmap.' The chapter does hedge ('any list will date itself fast'), limiting damage.  
  _scope:_ ch23-multimodal (frontier VLM / voice sections)  
  _evidence:_ ch23 L215 'Claude 3.5 / 4 with vision ... Gemini 1.5 / 2.0'; L235 'Claude voice (Anthropic, on the roadmap) and other variants are converging'; L35/L215/L220 stamped 'as of early 2025' / 'as of 2025'.  
  _fix:_ Lean on the existing hedge: replace point-version pairs with family names ('Claude's vision-capable models', 'Google's Gemini line') and change 'Claude voice (on the roadmap)' to 'voice-native models from Anthropic and others are shipping.' Keep the 'as of early 2025' stamp so the snapshot is dated.
- **[minor]** DeepSeek-V3 is called 'the current open-weights state of the art on most benchmarks.' It is stamped 'as of late 2024,' so it ages acceptably, but by mid-2026 it has been superseded by later DeepSeek releases and other open-weights models (Llama-4, Qwen3, etc.), so the unqualified 'state of the art' reads stale.  
  _scope:_ ch11-moe (DeepSeek-V3 section)  
  _evidence:_ ch11 L295 'DeepSeek-V3 ... It is, as of late 2024, the current open-weights state of the art on most benchmarks.'  
  _fix:_ Soften to 'was, as of late 2024, among the strongest open-weights models' and frame it as the architectural exemplar (fine-grained + shared experts, aux-loss-free balancing) rather than a live leaderboard claim, so the technical point survives leaderboard churn.
- **[minor]** Frontier examples are pinned to the GPT-4 generation: 'Frontier models (Claude Opus, GPT-4o, Gemini Pro)' and 'Small models (Claude Haiku, GPT-4 mini).' By mid-2026 the GPT-4o/GPT-4-mini tier is no longer frontier (GPT-5 generation exists). Stamped 'Typical 2025 cost-quality tradeoffs,' so it ages, but the named tier is dated.  
  _scope:_ ch30-agent-eval-and-frameworks (cost-quality section); ch13-sft aside  
  _evidence:_ ch30 L162 'Small models (Claude Haiku, GPT-4 mini) ... Frontier models (Claude Opus, GPT-4o, Gemini Pro)'; ch13 L13 lists 'GPT-4, Claude, Llama-3-Instruct, Mistral-Instruct, Gemini' as the modern chat models.  
  _fix:_ Prefer tier language over point versions: 'a cheap/small tier (Claude Haiku-class, GPT-mini-class) vs a frontier tier (flagship Claude/GPT/Gemini)'. The cost-quality argument is version-independent; only the illustrative names need to be generic.
- **[minor]** Currency-hardening is already largely in place: chapters 14, 20, 22, 23, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29, 30 consistently stamp claims with 'as of early 2025' / 'as of late 2024' and add 'lists will date' caveats, and ch17 future-proofs with a range ('2024-2026'). The gap is that ch10's hardware section is the least consistently stamped (present-tense 'current workhorse') and is where the missing-generation problem bites hardest.  
  _scope:_ Whole-book meta-observation (positive, with one gap)  
  _evidence:_ grep 'as of early 2025' hits ch14/23/25/26/27/28/29/30; ch17 L286 'the default starting point for self-hosted serving in 2024-2026'; contrast ch10 L39 'the current workhorse' (unqualified present tense).  
  _fix:_ Adopt one house convention repo-wide: every leaderboard/hardware/'state-of-the-art' claim gets an explicit 'as of <date>' stamp plus a 'this will move' caveat, as most chapters already do. Apply it to ch10's accelerator and pricing paragraphs, which currently read as timeless.

### Runnable numpy code correctness audit across all 30 chapters (src/pages/ch*/index.mdx)
I extracted all 157 `defaultCode` RunnableCode blocks across the 30 chapters (88 numpy, 69 pure-Python) and executed every one with python3+numpy. None raise at runtime; the completed numpy demos are overwhelmingly correct — softmax/cross-entropy, scaled-dot-product and multi-head attention, the RoPE relative-position invariant (numerically verified: all four offset-5 dot products equal 0.164293), ALiBi bias, LayerNorm normalization, sinusoidal PE range, MinHash Jaccard, gradient clipping, and ring/data-parallel all-reduce all produce what the prose claims, and exercise scaffolds are intentionally unimplemented `pass` stubs. The one substantive defect is in Chapter 9: the compute-optimal Chinchilla demo prints D/N ratios of 320–696 while both its inline comment and the chapter's central 'rule of thumb' say ~20, caused by inconsistent closed-form constants (k_N=0.6, k_D=1.7 with a non-0.5 exponent). Three minor issues round it out: a sampling demo whose narration promises 'occasional alternates' that never appear, an AdamW demo whose 'exactly 3'/'tugs toward 0' narration doesn't match its displayed 3.0378, and two torch RunnableCode blocks that cannot execute in the browser's Pyodide runtime (one also has an empty packages list despite importing torch). Note: the RuntimeWarnings about 'divide by zero/overflow in matmul' seen locally are false positives from macOS Accelerate BLAS under numpy 2.0 and do not occur in the Pyodide runtime the site actually uses — not a content bug.

- **[major]** The runnable demo prints a D/N (tokens-per-parameter) ratio of 320–696, directly contradicting its own inline comment 'note the D/N ratio sits near 20' (line 106) and the chapter's headline claim that the Chinchilla optimum is 'D_opt/N_opt ≈ 20, about 20 training tokens per parameter' (line 75). A reader who runs the block sees hundreds, not ~20.  
  _scope:_ ch09-scaling-and-distributed/index.mdx — the compute-optimal Chinchilla RunnableCode demo (block at line 85, function at line 97)  
  _evidence:_ Executed the block: output rows are `1.0e+22  N=2.30e+09  D=7.38e+11  D/N=320.6`, rising to `3.0e+25 ... D/N=695.9`. Root cause: `compute_optimal` (lines 97-101) uses `a = BETA/(ALPHA+BETA) = 0.4516`, `N_opt = 0.6*(C/6)**a`, `D_opt = 1.7*(C/6)**(1-a)`, so `D/N = (1.7/0.6)*(C/6)**(1-2a) = 2.83*(C/6)**0.097`, which is hundreds, never ~20. Constants k_N=0.6, k_D=1.7 and the non-0.5 exponent are internally inconsistent with the stated rule.  
  _fix:_ Make the closed form yield a near-constant ~20: either use symmetric exponent 0.5 for both N and D (N,D ∝ sqrt(C/6)) with constants chosen so k_D/k_N ≈ 20, or replace the closed form with the r-sweep the exercise already uses (line 419: sweep r=D/N over logspace and pick the loss-minimizing r), which correctly recovers r≈20. Re-run so the printed D/N column matches the prose and the line-106 comment.
- **[minor]** The block's own printed narration claims 'Mostly token 5 (the model's confident choice), with occasional alternates from the top of the distribution' (lines 615-616), but with the fixed seed and these parameters (logit[5]=5.0, T=0.7, top_p=0.9) all 10 samples are token 5 — there are never any alternates.  
  _scope:_ ch08-building-small-llm/index.mdx — top-p/temperature sampling demo (block at line 572, sample_next_token at line 580)  
  _evidence:_ Ran the block: output is 'sample 1..10: token 5' for all ten. At T=0.7 token 5's softmax prob exceeds 0.9, so the nucleus collapses to {5} every step; np.random.seed(42) makes this deterministic. The narrated 'occasional alternates' never occurs.  
  _fix:_ Either lower logit[5] (e.g. 2.5) or raise temperature/top_p so the nucleus actually contains alternates and the 10 samples show variety, or soften the printed text to state that with this confident distribution the sampler returns token 5 every time.
- **[minor]** The printed conclusion 'With weight_decay = 0 the converged value would be exactly 3' (line 263) is inaccurate, and the displayed result (x=3.0378) sits above 3, so it does not visibly demonstrate the claimed 'weight decay tugs slightly toward 0' (line 262).  
  _scope:_ ch08-building-small-llm/index.mdx — AdamW demo (block at line 224)  
  _evidence:_ Ran extended: with wd=0 the value settles at ~3.0035 (not exactly 3 — Adam's constant-magnitude step and the +eps denominator leave a residual), and with wd=0.05 the equilibrium is ~2.996. At the demo's 50 steps x=3.0378 (still descending from 5, above 3), so the printed value is on the wrong side of 3 to show the decay-toward-0 effect.  
  _fix:_ Run more steps (e.g. 200) so the displayed value lands near equilibrium (wd=0.05 → ~2.996, below 3, matching the narration), and reword 'exactly 3' to 'very close to 3'.
- **[minor]** These are executable RunnableCode widgets whose code begins with `import torch`, but the browser Pyodide runtime has no PyTorch wheel, so clicking Run raises ModuleNotFoundError. The line-439 block additionally declares packages={[]}, so even the intended dependency is absent.  
  _scope:_ ch08-building-small-llm/index.mdx — the two PyTorch training RunnableCode blocks (block at line 439 with packages={[]}; block at line 835 with packages={["torch"]})  
  _evidence:_ Block at mdx line 439 imports torch (lines 446-448) yet its packages attribute (line 515) is `packages={[]}`; block at line 835 imports torch/nn/functional and declares `packages={["torch"]}` (line 927), but torch is not installable in Pyodide. Both are illustrative 'Assumes: model ...' snippets never meant to fully run, but they are still rendered with a Run button that will error.  
  _fix:_ Either mark these as non-runnable static code fences (drop RunnableCode) since torch cannot run in-browser, or add a leading comment making clear the block is reference-only; at minimum fix the line-439 packages list to match its imports.

### Pedagogical arc & coherence audit across all 30 chapters (src/pages/ch*/index.mdx) + context/CURRICULUM.md Connections and src/pages/index.astro. Read every chapter intro and closing/exercises, mapped the full builds-on/sets-up dependency graph, and grep-audited cross-chapter notation (sequence length, model dim, layer/parameter symbols), chapter cross-references, and topic redundancy.
The dependency spine is fundamentally sound: nearly every chapter honors its stated prerequisites and hands off cleanly to its dependents (e.g. Ch13→14→15→16 post-training arc, Ch16→17→18→19 inference trilogy, Ch27→28→29→30 agent arc all point forward correctly and to the right topics), and heavy foundations (backprop/autograd, cross-entropy gradient) are referenced rather than re-derived in Ch8. The problems are not missing dependencies but inconsistent surface conventions layered on a correct skeleton. Three arc-level issues dominate: (1) two mismatched section-numbering systems run in parallel — the site and curriculum say "Part I–IX" while chapter prose (Ch10–30, 169 occurrences) uses an undefined "Phase 9–15", where Phase N = Part (N−6); (2) Chapter 4, the pivotal attention chapter, carries stale cross-references from an earlier chapter numbering (calls the transformer block "Chapter 7" and state-space models "Chapter 11", both wrong); (3) sequence-length notation drifts across chapters (n / T / N / "seq") and the symbol N is overloaded three ways (parameters in Ch9, layers in Ch8, sequence length in Ch12/17). Redundancy is minor and mostly well-managed, with two spots (FlashAttention, NF4 quantization) taught twice without a linking cross-reference. No true pedagogical gaps (concepts used but never taught) were found.

- **[major]** Two parallel, mismatched section-numbering taxonomies. The reader-facing homepage renders sections as 'Part {num}' (Part I–IX) and CURRICULUM.md's index table uses 'Part', but chapter prose from Ch10 onward narrates the arc in terms of 'Phase 9' through 'Phase 15'. The mapping is Phase N = Part (N−6): e.g. Ch17–19 prose says 'Phase 12' but the site calls it 'Part VI — Inference'; Ch20 says 'Phase 13 opens here' but the site calls it 'Part VII'. 'Phase' is never defined for the reader and is absent from Ch1–9 entirely, so it appears abruptly at Ch10 as leaked internal build/session scaffolding.  
  _scope:_ Ch10–30 prose (21 chapters, 169 occurrences) vs. index.astro + CURRICULUM.md  
  _evidence:_ index.astro: `<div class="part-card-eyebrow">Part {part.num}</div>`. `grep -rhoE 'Phase [0-9]+' ch*/index.mdx | wc -l` = 169 across 21 files (ch10–ch30). ch17 intro: 'Phase 11 left you with a trained model... Phase 12 asks'. ch20 intro: 'Phase 13 opens here.' ch16:640 'Phase 11 is complete'. Ch1–9 have zero 'Phase' occurrences. CURRICULUM.md line 1581 'Phase' table is about build sessions (Scaffolding/Pre-research), not these content phases — so the term is doubly ambiguous.  
  _fix:_ Pick one taxonomy and apply it everywhere the reader sees. Cleanest: global find/replace 'Phase N' → the matching 'Part <roman>' (or the part's name) throughout Ch10–30 prose so chapter narration matches the nav the reader is looking at. If 'Phase' is intentionally kept, define it once early and renumber so Phase and Part numbers coincide.
- **[major]** Stale forward cross-references inherited from an earlier chapter numbering, now pointing to the wrong chapters. Ch4 twice calls the transformer block 'Chapter 7' (it is Chapter 5; Chapter 7 is Pre-training data), and twice calls state-space models 'Chapter 11' (they are Chapter 12; Chapter 11 is MoE). The same chapter correctly says multi-head attention is 'Chapter 5', so the errors are internally self-contradictory. This mislabels the two most important structural payoffs of the attention chapter.  
  _scope:_ Ch4 (attention) — the pivotal foundational chapter every later chapter is 'downstream of'  
  _evidence:_ ch04-attention/index.mdx:29 'The operation lives inside the transformer block (Chapter 7)'; :558 'Chapter 7 wraps attention in the transformer block. Chapter 11 introduces an alternative that trades the formula for linear scaling' (linear-scaling alternative = SSM = Ch12, not the MoE Ch11); :377 'Chapter 11 introduces state-space models... with O(n) scaling'. Contrast :385 which correctly cites 'Chapter 5 derives all of this' for multi-head. No other chapter mislabels the block (ch06:11 'block from Chapter 5', ch08:21 'block (Chapter 5)').  
  _fix:_ In ch04, change 'transformer block (Chapter 7)' → 'Chapter 5' (lines 29, 558) and 'Chapter 11' → 'Chapter 12' for the state-space/linear-scaling references (lines 377, 558). Sweep ch04 for any other refs assuming the old split (block==7, SSM==11).
- **[major]** Sequence length is denoted by a different symbol in almost every chapter, and the symbol N is overloaded across three distinct meanings. Sequence length: 'n' in Ch4/Ch5 (O(n^2)), 'T' in Ch8 (T=1024) and partly Ch12, 'N' in Ch12/Ch17 (O(N^2), N=8192), and literal 'seq' in Ch17 (O(seq^2)). Meanwhile N separately means number of parameters in Ch9's scaling law (C≈6ND) and number of stacked transformer blocks/layers in Ch8 ('block stacked N times'). A reader who learns N=parameters in Ch9 then meets O(N^2) attention in Ch17 must silently re-map the same letter. No chapter uses 'L', so the reader is never given a stable anchor.  
  _scope:_ Cross-chapter notation: Ch4, Ch5, Ch8, Ch9, Ch12, Ch17 (sequence-length and the symbol N)  
  _evidence:_ ch04 O(n^2)/O(n^2 d); ch05 O(n^2·d_model); ch08 'sequence length T = 1024', and O(K^2)/O(K) for K generated tokens, plus intro 'block stacked $N$ times'; ch12 O(N^2), O(T), O(T log T); ch17 O(N^2), O(N), and O(seq^2)/O(seq); ch09 'model of size $N$ on $D$ tokens costs C≈6ND' (N=params, 6 occurrences of 6ND). So seq-len ∈ {n, T, N, seq} and N ∈ {params, layers, seq-len}.  
  _fix:_ Add a one-page notation table to the front matter / Ch1 and enforce it globally: e.g. reserve one symbol for sequence length (n or T), 'L' for number of layers, 'N' for parameter count, d_model / d_k as already used. Then normalize O(...) expressions and prose in Ch4,5,8,12,17 to that convention; eliminate the literal 'seq' in Ch17.
- **[minor]** The FlashAttention I/O-complexity argument (materializing the N×N score matrix costs O(N^2) HBM traffic; tiling + online softmax in SRAM makes it O(N) memory) is derived essentially twice, in Ch10 for training kernels and again in Ch17 for inference serving, with no cross-reference linking the two treatments. The lens differs (training vs. serving) so full removal isn't warranted, but the reader isn't told they've seen the core argument before.  
  _scope:_ Ch10 (§6 Custom kernels) and Ch17 (Flash Attention section)  
  _evidence:_ ch10 has 24 'FlashAttention' mentions incl. §'Custom kernels: Triton and FlashAttention' (line 199) with the tiling/SRAM I/O argument (lines 57, 207, 221). ch17 has 12 mentions incl. §'Flash Attention' (line 172) re-deriving 'tiling + online softmax', 'O(N^2)→O(N)', HBM round trips. Neither section says 'as covered in Ch10/see Ch17'.  
  _fix:_ Have Ch17's Flash Attention section explicitly reference the Ch10 derivation (or vice versa) and state what is new in the inference framing, so it reads as a recall + extension rather than a second independent derivation.
- **[minor]** Quantization is taught out of order and twice. Ch15 gives a fairly complete treatment of NF4 quantization, double quantization, and dequant-on-forward for QLoRA; Ch18 then teaches quantization formally and back-references Ch15 ('NF4, which you saw in Chapter 15'). But Ch15 itself never forward-points to Ch18, so a reader meeting 4-bit NF4 for the first time in Ch15 isn't told the full quantization treatment is coming. Curriculum acknowledges this ordering tension (Ch15 'Sets up Ch18').  
  _scope:_ Ch15 (PEFT/QLoRA) → Ch18 (Quantization)  
  _evidence:_ ch18:15 'NF4, which you saw in Chapter 15 as the quantization layer of QLoRA'. ch15 lines 249–261, 317 teach NF4/double-quant/paged optimizers in depth; `grep 'chapter 18|quantiz' ch15` shows no reference to Chapter 18 anywhere in ch15.  
  _fix:_ Add a one-line forward pointer in Ch15's QLoRA/NF4 section: 'quantization is covered in depth in Chapter 18; here we use only what QLoRA needs.' Optionally trim Ch15's NF4 mechanics to the minimum QLoRA requires and defer the rest to Ch18 to reduce duplication.

### WIDGET & RUNNABLE inventory: CURRICULUM.md specs vs built widgets (src/components/widgets/) and RunnableCode/widget usages in src/pages/chXX/index.mdx, all 30 chapters (read-only)
The built widget system is high quality and internally consistent: 62 widget .tsx files exist, every one is referenced in its chapter MDX (zero orphans), there are no stubs or placeholders (smallest widget is 121 lines; all "placeholder"/"lorem ipsum" grep hits are legitimate domain data), and spot-checked widget data is factually correct (ch11 Mixtral 46.7B/12.9B and DeepSeek-V3 671B/37B match reality). Every chapter ships at least as many widgets as its CURRICULUM spec, usually one extra, so no specced widget capability is genuinely absent. However, CURRICULUM.md is not an accurate inventory of what was built: the one-line "Widgets" specs are renamed or split relative to the shipped components in roughly half the chapters, and one specced widget (ch28 AgentStateMachineDiagram) has no built equivalent. Separately, several specced "Pyodide-executable" runnables that require the live Anthropic API (ch21/26/27/28/30) cannot run as specced because RunnableCode is Pyodide-only with no network support, so they ship as static schematic code; and chapters the curriculum labels "None runnable" (notably ch10) actually ship multiple working RunnableCode blocks. The build over-delivers; the CURRICULUM inventory is simply stale, which is expected since the file self-declares that full specs live in prompts/chapters/ (the real source of truth, which the widgets do match).

- **[major]** CURRICULUM widget one-liners are systematically out of sync with the built widgets (renamed, split, or in one case never built), so the file cannot be used as a widget inventory. Only ch28 is a genuine missing-widget case: the specced 'AgentStateMachineDiagram' does not exist and no state-machine diagram widget or prose was built; the session prompt superseded it with AgentTraceInspector + ToolSchemaBuilder.  
  _scope:_ CURRICULUM.md 'Widgets' specs vs src/components/widgets/ across ~15 chapters; ch28 is the one true gap  
  _evidence:_ Renamed/split mismatches: ch2 EmbeddingSpaceExplorer->EmbeddingSpace; ch3 TokenizerPlayground->BPETraining+TokenizerComparison; ch5 TransformerBlockDissection->TransformerBlockFlow; ch6 PositionEncodingVisualizer->SinusoidalPE+RoPERotation; ch8 LossCurveInteractive->LossCurve; ch13 ChatTemplateVisualizer->ChatTemplateComparison; ch15 LoRARankVisualizer->LoRAArchitecture+ParameterBudgetCalculator; ch16 TemperatureScalingVisualizer->TemperatureScaling; ch19 SamplingDistributionInteractive->SamplingDistribution; ch21 ToolCallTraceViewer->ToolCallTrace; ch27 AgentLoopTrace->AgenticLoopVisualizer; ch29 MultiAgentTopologySelector->MultiAgentTopologyExplorer. ch28: CURRICULUM.md:1457 specs 'AgentStateMachineDiagram'; grep for state.?machine/StateMachine in src/pages/ch28-agent-from-scratch/index.mdx returns nothing; prompts/chapters/ch28/session-123-agent-trace-inspector...md builds AgentTraceInspector + ToolSchemaBuilder instead.  
  _fix:_ Treat CURRICULUM.md widget lines as non-authoritative (they already declare prompts/ as source of truth) or refresh them to shipped component names. For ch28, either add the promised state-machine diagram or update CURRICULUM.md:1457 to AgentTraceInspector + ToolSchemaBuilder.
- **[major]** CURRICULUM specs these as Pyodide-executable runnables where the user supplies an API key and a fetch is made via JS bridge (ch21 tool_loop_anthropic.py, ch26 llm_judge.py, ch27 react_loop.py, ch28 agent_harness.py, ch30 custom_agent_eval.py). RunnableCode is Pyodide-only with no network/fetch/API-key/localStorage support, so these cannot execute as specified; they ship as static schematic code.  
  _scope:_ Runnable code specs for ch21, ch26, ch27, ch28, ch30 (live-Anthropic-API runnables)  
  _evidence:_ src/components/code/RunnableCode.tsx imports only runPython from '@lib/pyodide'; grep for fetch/api-key/anthropic/network/XMLHttpRequest in RunnableCode.tsx returns nothing. src/pages/ch21-tool-use/index.mdx line 59 code comment: 'Anthropic-style tool call structure (schematic, no API calls).' CURRICULUM.md:1119 (ch21) promises 'Pyodide can run this; the fetch call is made via JS bridge' plus an API-key input form that was not built.  
  _fix:_ Reconcile spec with reality: update the ch21/26/27/28/30 runnable specs in CURRICULUM to state these are static/schematic reference code, or implement the JS-bridge + API-key-in-localStorage capability in RunnableCode.
- **[minor]** CURRICULUM claims these chapters have essentially no runnable code, but the built chapters ship multiple working RunnableCode blocks. Positive over-delivery, but it contradicts the curriculum, so the doc understates what exists.  
  _scope:_ ch10 (and to a lesser degree ch9) 'Runnable code' spec  
  _evidence:_ CURRICULUM.md:548 (ch10): 'None runnable. This chapter is reference-heavy... Static reference snippets only.' Actual: src/pages/ch10-training-infra/index.mdx has 8 <RunnableCode> blocks (numpy / pure-python). ch9 CURRICULUM.md:502 says distributed is shown statically, yet the chapter ships 7-8 runnable blocks.  
  _fix:_ Update the ch10 (and ch9) runnable-code notes in CURRICULUM.md to reflect that conceptual illustrations were made runnable, or accept the doc understates scope.
- **[minor]** No defects found in these categories. Recorded as a negative result so the audit is complete: no orphan widgets, no stub/placeholder widgets, and spot-checked widget data matches the prose and known facts.  
  _scope:_ Orphan widgets / stub widgets / prose contradictions — all three checks came back clean  
  _evidence:_ 62 widget .tsx files, all 62 referenced by a matching <Widget client:...> usage in the chapter MDX (orphan-check loop printed none). Smallest widget SFTLossMasking.tsx = 121 lines; grep for TODO/FIXME/stub/'coming soon'/'not implemented' returned only legitimate domain content (ch7 'placeholder' doc category + lorem-ipsum test doc; ch24 payload placeholder tokens). Factual spot-check: ch11 model-data.ts Mixtral 8x7B 46.7e9/12.9e9 and DeepSeek-V3 671e9/37e9 both correct.  
  _fix:_ No action needed; widget engineering quality is strong and consistent across all 30 chapters.

### Voice/tone consistency audit across all 30 chapters (src/pages/ch*/index.mdx) against context/PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md — "Tone and voice", "Specifically forbidden phrases", "Headings name the thing". Read-only. Em-dash / comma-splice issues excluded per instructions.
The explicitly-forbidden phrase list is clean: zero hits for "In this section/chapter, we will", "It's important to note", "As we can see", "It's worth mentioning", "Without further ado", "Stay tuned", "Hopefully this clarifies", and no first-person-singular slips (the only "Let me" is inside a legitimate in-example ReAct trace). The real problems are structural and concentrated in the back third of the book. First, authoring-spec directives leaked verbatim into reader-facing prose in the late intros ("The tonal frame is engineering with empathy for the LLM", "Honest about what's not covered. Forward-looking without predicting. The closing thought sends the reader off well"). Second, a pervasive "The next section covers X…" announce-the-next-sentence transition appears ~128 times across ~25 chapters — exactly the pattern the spec bans. Third, there is a stark tonal drift: staccato bold-fragment rhetoric is essentially absent in Ch 1–5 (0–9 bold spans) and explodes by Ch 27–30 (167–187 bold spans, 58–73 of them whole-sentence bolds), accompanied by curriculum-countdown meta-fragments ("Four chapters from the end.", "One chapter remains.", "Now let's finish.") whose only function is to announce position. A handful of "How X works" gerund headings also violate the "name the thing" rule.

- **[blocker]** Authoring-spec / self-instruction notes leaked verbatim into reader-facing prose. These sentences describe the author's intent for the chapter rather than teaching the reader anything, and read as un-cleaned build scaffolding.  
  _scope:_ ch28-agent-from-scratch, ch30-agent-eval-and-frameworks (chapter intros)  
  _evidence:_ ch28-agent-from-scratch/index.mdx:15 'The tonal frame is engineering with empathy for the LLM.' and again ch28:33 'The chapter's tonal frame is engineering with empathy for the LLM.'; ch30-agent-eval-and-frameworks/index.mdx:15 'Honest about what's not covered. Forward-looking without predicting. The closing thought sends the reader off well.'  
  _fix:_ Delete the meta/authoring-note sentences. If the tone point is worth keeping, express it as content (e.g. state the tool-design principle directly) rather than announcing the chapter's 'tonal frame'. Cut 'The closing thought sends the reader off well' entirely — it narrates the author's plan, not the material.
- **[major]** Systematic 'The next section covers/introduces/is…' end-of-section transition. This is the 'any sentence whose only function is to announce the next sentence' pattern the spec explicitly forbids ('The reader can see the section heading'). Many carry a scrap of framing, but the pure form is a bare announcement.  
  _scope:_ ~25 of 30 chapters; ch05/12/15/17/19/21/23/24/25/26/27 densest (6-8 each). ~128 total occurrences  
  _evidence:_ grep -cE 'The next (section|subsection)' totals 128. Pure-announce examples: ch15-peft/index.mdx:241 'The next section covers QLoRA, the combination of 4-bit quantization and LoRA…'; ch21-tool-use/index.mdx:35,161,262,280 (four in one chapter); ch19-sampling/index.mdx:218,256,345; ch17-inference-optimization/index.mdx:33,53,188.  
  _fix:_ Delete the bare 'The next section covers…' sentences and let the heading do the work. Where the transition carries a real idea (e.g. ch02:319 'The next section is the corrective.', ch06:193 'the chapter's destination'), fold that idea into the preceding paragraph's argument instead of pointing forward.
- **[major]** Staccato bold-fragment rhetoric escalates sharply from front to back, breaking the 'one voice' consistency. Early chapters are clean unadorned prose; late chapters machine-gun bold declarative fragments for emphasis, drifting toward the 'marketing prose' the spec bans.  
  _scope:_ Tonal drift across the whole book: Ch 1-5 vs Ch 27-30  
  _evidence:_ Bold-span counts per chapter climb monotonically: ch01=0, ch02=0, ch04=0, ch03=5, ch05=9 … ch27=167, ch29=167, ch30=187. Whole-sentence bolds (**…period.**): ch30=73, ch29=58, ch28=30, ch27=24, vs ~0 in Ch1-5. Representative: ch29-multi-agent/index.mdx:15, ch30-agent-eval-and-frameworks/index.mdx:15, ch28-agent-from-scratch/index.mdx:11-15.  
  _fix:_ In Ch 21-30 intros and section leads, demote most whole-sentence bolds to plain prose; reserve bold for genuine term-introduction. Target the front-chapter density (a few bolds per chapter, none as standalone sentence fragments) so the voice reads as one author throughout.
- **[major]** Curriculum-countdown meta-narration: fragments whose only function is to announce reading position or the act of closing. Direct instance of the forbidden announce-the-next-sentence rule, plus 'Now let's finish.' is the imperative-let's construction the spec bans ('Let's dive in').  
  _scope:_ ch23, ch25, ch26, ch27, ch28, ch29, ch30 (closing/opening 'curriculum' passages)  
  _evidence:_ ch27-agent-foundations/index.mdx:15 'Four chapters from the end.' + heading ch27:859 'Four chapters remain'; ch28-agent-from-scratch/index.mdx:15 'Three chapters from the end.' + heading ch28:937 'Three chapters from the end'; ch29-multi-agent/index.mdx:15 'One chapter remains.' /:826 'One chapter from the end.' + heading ch29:803 'One chapter remains'; ch30:15 'Now let's finish.'; ch30:341 'Section 8 looks back…', ch30:341 'Section 7 takes stock…' announce sections by number.  
  _fix:_ Cut the countdown fragments and 'Now let's finish.'. Cross-chapter pointers should follow the spec's explicit form ('Chapter 30 evaluates agents at scale, see §30.x') and carry information, not a position counter. Rename countdown headings to name their actual content.
- **[minor]** Table-of-contents narration in intros: bold-fragment lists that preview the section structure the reader will see in the headings anyway. Spec: 'The reader can see the section heading. They don't need to be told what's coming.'  
  _scope:_ ch28:13, ch29:15 (and milder at ch06:43)  
  _evidence:_ ch28-agent-from-scratch/index.mdx:13 'The work breaks into six concerns. Tool design… Tool implementation… Schemas… Error handling… Observability… Scaffolding…'; ch29-multi-agent/index.mdx:15 'Eight sections. When multi-agent is warranted. Three architectures… Communication patterns… Role specialization…'  
  _fix:_ Replace the section-list preview with one or two sentences of actual argument that motivate the chapter. Drop the 'Eight sections.' / 'six concerns' framing entirely.
- **[minor]** 'How X works' headings name the act of explaining rather than the thing — a direct match to the spec's ❌ example 'How the KV cache works'.  
  _scope:_ ch09-scaling-and-distributed, ch13-sft  
  _evidence:_ ch09-scaling-and-distributed/index.mdx:141 '### How DP works', ch09:257 '### How FSDP works', ch13-sft/index.mdx:71 '### How the mask works'.  
  _fix:_ Rename to name the mechanism itself, e.g. 'Data parallelism', 'FSDP sharding', 'The loss mask' — matching the spec's 'Multi-head attention' / 'The KV cache' heading convention.
- **[minor]** Marketing-flavored 'you'll have the full stack / this is the foundation every product rests on' framing drifts toward the promotional register the spec forbids ('No marketing prose'). Milder than the bold-drift finding but same direction; note the front chapters use the restrained 'By the end, the reader should be able to…' form instead.  
  _scope:_ ch21, ch22, ch28, ch30 (chapter openers)  
  _evidence:_ ch30-agent-eval-and-frameworks/index.mdx:15 'By the end of this chapter you'll have the full stack of an LLM systems engineer'; ch28-agent-from-scratch/index.mdx:15 'the operational toolkit to build agents that hold up in production'; ch21-tool-use/index.mdx:32 'The patterns in this chapter are not optional knowledge for engineers building AI systems; they are the foundation.'; ch22-retrieval-and-rag/index.mdx:36 'If you are building AI products, you are building (or about to build) a RAG system'.  
  _fix:_ Rephrase to the measured, capability-specific form used in Ch 3-5 ('By the end, the reader should be able to compute the float-to-int mapping…'). State what the reader can do, not how foundational/economically-consequential the topic is.

---

## Per-chapter findings

### Chapter 1 — grade B+

A strong, mathematically careful chapter: the cross-entropy and matrix-form MLP derivations are correct line-by-line, every cross-reference is accurate, the runnable numpy is correct and would produce the described outputs, and it ships three working widgets against one specced. It is pulled down by two genuine technical errors in explanatory prose (the SGD step-direction on the elongated quadratic is reversed; the Llama FFN-bias claim is false) and one explicitly-specced Key concept — the death-of-ReLU phenomenon — being entirely absent. The remaining issues are minor polish.

**Strengths:** Core derivations are correct and rigorous: the cross-entropy-gradient proof (softmax Jacobian, one-hot cancellation to p-y) and the matrix-form 2-layer backward pass with shape annotations both match the research derivations exactly.; Cross-reference integrity is clean: all Chapter refs (2 embeddings, 5 transformer block, 8 small LLM) and all internal 'section N' references resolve to the correct targets.; Runnable code is correct and executable: stable softmax, the quadrant-task MLP training loop (correct grad/param pairing, in-place SGD update, log(4)->0 loss story), the Adam optimizer, and the micrograd Value class all run as claimed.; Widgets exceed spec (3 vs 1 specced) and compute correctly: BackpropVisualizer, TrainingCurves, and AutogradGraph all reproduce the chapter's forward/backward math (verified finalGrad values a=-3,b=2,c=1 in the trace).; Exercises are meaningful and well-scaffolded (softmax stability, 3-hidden-layer extension, AdamW-from-Adam, autograd engine), with correct hints and expected outputs; not filler. Voice matches the house style with effective intuition pumps (fog/hillside, recipe-in-reverse).

**Findings:**

- **[major/technical-error/CONFIRMED]** For f(x,y)=x^2+10y^2 the gradient is [2x, 20y]; the y-direction is the steep, high-curvature one, so plain SGD takes LARGER steps in y and SMALLER steps in x — the reverse of what the prose states. This teaches the ill-conditioning intuition backwards (the classic behavior is small progress along the shallow x-axis, overshoot/oscillation along the steep y-axis).
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:465 — "Plain SGD on this loss takes large $x$-steps and small $y$-steps. Adam normalizes them."
  - _fix:_ Swap the axes: 'Plain SGD takes large y-steps (the steep direction, gradient 20y) and small x-steps (gradient 2x), so it oscillates in y while crawling in x. Adam normalizes both to a uniform step.'
- **[major/technical-error/CONFIRMED]** Llama (LLaMA/Llama 2/Llama 3) uses bias=False on ALL linear layers, including the SwiGLU FFN (w1, w2, w3) — it does not keep biases in the FFN. Citing the Llama family as the reference for 'omit attention bias but keep FFN bias' is factually wrong; models that drop attention biases (Llama, PaLM) drop FFN biases too, and this 'keep-FFN-bias' pattern is not a real modern convention.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:50 — "They keep biases in the FFN layers. The Llama family is the standard reference for this pattern."
  - _fix:_ Correct to: modern LLMs like Llama and PaLM drop biases from all linear projections (attention and FFN); GPT-2/GPT-3 keep them everywhere. Remove the false 'keep biases in FFN' attribution.
- **[major/missing-depth/CONFIRMED]** The curriculum lists "the 'death of ReLU' phenomenon" as an explicit Key concept, and research MC4 flags it as a common misconception to correct. The chapter never mentions dying/dead ReLU neurons (a neuron whose pre-activation is negative for every example, so its gradient is permanently zero). GELU/SiLU are introduced as 'smooth ReLU-likes' without the key motivation — that they keep a nonzero gradient for negative inputs and thereby avoid dead units.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:41 — ReLU discussion ("It induces sparsity: about half of the activations ... are exactly zero") / line 43 GELU-SiLU intro
  - _fix:_ Add a short paragraph or callout on dead ReLUs (permanently-zero neurons) in the activation section, and note that leaky/GELU/SiLU address it — matching the specced Key concept.
- **[minor/code-bug/CONFIRMED]** In the Adam demo, x,y are unpacked BEFORE opt.step() but the printed loss (using those pre-step x,y) is displayed alongside params[0] which is printed AFTER the step. The reported loss is therefore for the point one step behind the reported x,y (e.g. step 0 prints x=y=4.900 but loss=275.0=5^2+10*5^2, not 264.1). A reader recomputing x^2+10y^2 from the shown coordinates gets a different number.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:494 — "x, y = params[0]" then line 498 "loss = x**2 + 10 * y**2"
  - _fix:_ Compute the loss from params[0] after the step (or re-read x,y post-step) so the printed loss and coordinates are consistent.
- **[minor/pedagogy/CONFIRMED]** Both the section-8 autograd runnable and Exercise 4 use x=2,w=-3,b=1, giving x*w+b=-5 and relu(-5)=0, so EVERY gradient is 0. This is an anticlimactic verification: a reader cannot distinguish a correct autograd engine from one that always returns 0, because ReLU saturation zeros the mul/add backward rules before they're exercised. The AutogradGraph widget wisely uses relu(4)=4 with nonzero grads.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:565 — "L = (x * w + b).relu()" (also Exercise 4, line 748 "Expected: L = 0.0 ... So all grads should be 0 too.")
  - _fix:_ Pick inputs where relu is active (e.g. w=+3 so relu(7)=7), yielding nonzero dL/dx, dL/dw, dL/db that demonstrate the multiply/add rules actually firing.
- **[minor/widget-prose-mismatch/CONFIRMED]** The BackpropVisualizer operates on a single example (B=1) and labels nodes with scalar values and variable names (x[0], z1[0], a1[0], dz2[0]); it does not annotate tensor shapes (B×d_h, etc.). The prose promises 'shapes labeled at every step,' which the matrix-form section emphasizes, but the widget shows values, not shapes.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:275 — "forward pass, then backward pass, with shapes labeled at every step"
  - _fix:_ Reword the sentence to 'values labeled at every step' (or add shape annotations to the widget) to match what it actually renders.
- **[minor/pedagogy/CONFIRMED]** The demo logits [2.5,1.0,-0.5,3.2] give p=[0.30,0.07,0.02,0.61], so CE(target=3)=0.49 — the model is only 61% confident, and 0.49 nats is not 'near zero.' The 'confident-and-wrong is large' half holds (CE(target=2)=4.19), but the 'confident-and-right near zero' framing overstates this particular example.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:81 — "confident-and-right is near zero, confident-and-wrong is large"
  - _fix:_ Either soften to 'right-but-unsure is moderate, confidently-wrong is large,' or use a sharper logit vector (e.g. a clear peak) so the true-class loss is genuinely near zero.
- **[minor/coverage/PLAUSIBLE]** The research 'in scope and locked' list (research.md:29, TID2) specifies log-softmax via LogSumExp as a numerical-stability topic, and explicitly warns against composing softmax then log. The chapter covers the softmax max-trick thoroughly but never presents the log-softmax/LogSumExp identity; the training loop instead guards with an ad-hoc '+1e-12' (line 369). The softmax is stable so the demo is fine, but the specced LogSumExp content is absent.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch01-neural-net-primitives/index.mdx:92 — cross_entropy uses "p = softmax(logits)" then "return -np.log(p[target_class])"
  - _fix:_ Add a sentence and the identity log p_i = z_i - LogSumExp(z), noting this is what framework cross_entropy(logits, targets) does internally.

**Coverage gaps vs curriculum:**
- Death-of-ReLU / dying-ReLU phenomenon (explicitly listed in CURRICULUM Key concepts) is entirely missing; GELU/SiLU are introduced without the motivation that they fix dead neurons.
- log-softmax via LogSumExp (research 'in scope and locked', TID2) is not presented; the chapter's cross_entropy composes softmax then log and the training loop uses a +1e-12 guard instead.
- 'Read SGD, Adam, and AdamW ... and explain when each is preferred' is only half met — the chapter argues Adam is the default for transformers but never notes the counterpoint (research MC5) that SGD+momentum can give better final accuracy in some regimes, so the 'when each is preferred' objective is one-sided.
- The curriculum tricky spot 'gradient through a parameter vs gradient with respect to a parameter — autograd computes the latter' is not explicitly drawn anywhere in the autograd section.

**Exercises:** Four meaningful, correctly-scaffolded exercises spanning easy-to-hard (softmax numerical stability, extend MLP to 3 hidden layers, implement AdamW from Adam, build a micrograd-style Value engine). Hints and expected outputs are accurate (e.g. stable softmax of [1000,999,998] ≈ [0.665,0.244,0.090] verified; AdamW decoupled-decay hint correct). Not filler. One nit: Exercise 4 shares the all-zero-gradient example L=(x*w+b).relu()=0 with the section-8 runnable, so the 'verify by hand' payoff is a trivial all-zeros check rather than a demonstration of nonzero gradient flow.

**Widgets:** All three exported widgets exist and compute correctly. BackpropVisualizer (the sole specced widget) implements a correct B=1 forward/backward for a 2-16... (actually 2-4-3) MLP with hover tooltips showing the exact per-node math and the clean dz2=p-y. TrainingCurves correctly implements SGD/Momentum/Adam on the same quadrant task (D_H=16, D_OUT=4, He init, matching batch update math). AutogradGraph's TRACE reproduces the scalar chain rule exactly (verified finalGrads a=-3, b=2, c=1, d=e=L=1). Both extra widgets exceed the spec. Only mismatch: the prose claims the backprop widget labels 'shapes at every step,' but it labels scalar values and variable names, not tensor shapes.


### Chapter 2 — grade B+

A technically strong, well-voiced chapter: the SGNS math is derived correctly, all four runnable numpy blocks execute and produce their claimed outputs (I ran the skip-gram block: cat↔dog 0.552 > cat↔rug 0.05), internal section/EqRef cross-references are consistent, and the caveats around linear analogies are honest and appropriately hedged. The main defects are one confirmed factual error (the weight-tying examples name Llama and Mistral as tying when the 7B-class models do not), two exercises whose provided example test cases reference words absent from the corpora the reader is told to reuse (KeyError as written), and a handful of coverage gaps versus spec (no runnable king−man+woman demonstration on pretrained vectors, the distributional hypothesis / Firth never named). None of the core derivations or code are broken.

**Strengths:** SGNS objective and its gradients are derived correctly; I verified the sign conventions (pos_grad = σ(u·v)−1, neg_grad = σ(u·neg)) line by line and the runnable reproduces the claimed cat↔dog > cat↔rug result.; All four RunnableCode blocks are correct and runnable in Pyodide: EmbeddingLayer forward/backward with np.add.at, SGNS training, TinyLM bigram, TiedEmbedding — shapes and axes all check out.; Internal cross-references are clean: every 'section N' points to the right section, and <EqRef id="2.sgns"/> matches the labeled equation.; Honest, non-oversold treatment of linear analogies (the Levy-Goldberg PMI-factorization view is correctly flagged as hand-wavy, and the strict-evaluation critique is included).; Strong voice discipline — no forbidden phrases, headings name the thing, opinions owned by the prose; misconception callouts (MC1, MC2) are well-targeted.

**Findings:**

- **[blocker/technical-error/CONFIRMED]** Two of the four flagship examples are factually wrong. Llama (1/2/3 7B-class) and Mistral-7B do NOT tie input and output embeddings — their reference implementations use a separate `output`/`lm_head` matrix, and the HF configs set `tie_word_embeddings: false`. GPT-2 and Gemma do tie. If anything the pattern is the reverse of what the chapter implies: small models (GPT-2, Gemma-2B, some Llama-3.2 1B/3B) tie, while 7B+ open models (Llama-2-7B, Mistral-7B) commonly do not. The follow-on claim that GPT-3 does 'not tie' is also likely backwards (GPT-3 reuses the GPT-2 architecture, which ties). This misleads the reader about production practice in the very section devoted to it.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:428 — "GPT-2 ties. Llama ties. Mistral ties. Gemma ties."
  - _fix:_ Correct the examples: 'GPT-2 ties. Gemma ties. Many 7B+ models such as Llama-2-7B and Mistral-7B do NOT tie — the embedding is a small fraction of parameters at that scale, so the extra flexibility is cheap.' Reframe 'tie until you have a reason not to' as scale-dependent, and drop or verify the GPT-3 claim.
- **[major/exercise/CONFIRMED]** Exercise 2 tells the reader to use 'a trained embedding matrix E and word_to_id dict from section 4 or 6', but its example inputs use words (fish, car, red, blue, green) that are absent from both corpora. Section 4's vocab is {barked, cat, dog, mat, napped, on, purred, rug, sat, the}; section 6 is a–z plus space. `word_to_id['fish']` raises KeyError as written. The examples were evidently written against the widgets' animals/vehicles/foods/colors vocab, not section 4. Exercise 4's example `find_analogy('cat','dog','fish', ...)` has the same problem ('fish' not in section-4 vocab).
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:491 — "find_intruder(['cat', 'dog', 'fish', 'car'], E, word_to_id)" and :492 "['red', 'blue', 'green', 'cat']"
  - _fix:_ Either point these exercises at the 15-word widget corpus (animals/vehicles/foods) and provide it, or change the example words to ones in the section-4 vocab (e.g. intruder over ['cat','dog','sat','rug']). Make the referenced corpus consistent across Exercises 1–4.
- **[major/widget-prose-mismatch/CONFIRMED]** The Word2VecDynamics widget has no click-to-step interaction. Its controls are Reset, Play/Pause, and a scrubber (Word2VecDynamics.tsx lines 144–174), and the same WidgetFrame caption correctly says 'Drag the scrubber or hit Play'. The prose sentence contradicts both the widget and its own caption.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:277 — "Each click advances a step of SGD; positive pairs visibly pull together and negative pairs push apart"
  - _fix:_ Replace 'Each click advances a step of SGD' with 'Drag the scrubber or press Play to advance training' to match the actual controls.
- **[minor/widget-prose-mismatch/CONFIRMED]** The EmbeddingSpace widget's data (embedding-data.ts) is hand-authored 2D coordinates chosen so the analogy arrows are exactly parallel (e.g. every gender pair has delta (0,−2)), not a projection of real trained word2vec vectors. Calling them 'pre-computed word2vec embeddings' overstates; the caption more honestly says 'word2vec-like'. Also the prose caveat that 'the parallelism in the projection underestimates the structure' is inverted for synthetic data that is perfectly parallel.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:313 — "a 2D projection of pre-computed word2vec embeddings"
  - _fix:_ Say 'illustrative, word2vec-like 2D coordinates' in the prose to match the caption, or swap in a real GloVe PCA projection (which is what the curriculum specced).
- **[minor/currency/CONFIRMED]** The section-4 corpus has 21 whitespace-split tokens and 10 unique words (verified). The prose calls it a '22-word' corpus in section 4 and Exercise 1 calls the same corpus '12-word'. Neither number is right and they disagree with each other.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:206 — "trains SGNS on a 22-word toy corpus" and :440 "the 12-word corpus"
  - _fix:_ Use a consistent, correct descriptor, e.g. 'a 21-token corpus over a 10-word vocabulary', in both places.
- **[minor/stale-cross-ref/CONFIRMED]** Chapter 2 is in Part I — Foundations (CURRICULUM.md line 67: Part I is chapters 1–3). Contextualization is built in Part II — The Transformer. 'the rest of Part II' wrongly implies this chapter is already inside Part II.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:573 — "The fix is what the rest of Part II builds."
  - _fix:_ Change to 'The fix is what Part II builds.'
- **[minor/stale-cross-ref/CONFIRMED]** Chapter 4 is 'Attention mechanism'; the transformer block is assembled in Chapter 5. The embedded sequence first feeds attention in Ch 4, but 'the transformer from Chapter 4' is imprecise about where the block is built.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:15 — "the transformer from Chapter 4"
  - _fix:_ Say 'the attention stack introduced in Chapter 4' or 'the transformer built across Chapters 4–5'.
- **[minor/technical-error/CONFIRMED]** Word2vec is 2013; SimCLR is 2020 and CLIP is 2021, i.e. 7–8 years later, not 'half a decade' (5 years).
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:191 — "half a decade before SimCLR and CLIP made the framing standard"
  - _fix:_ Change to 'most of a decade before' or 'seven years before'.
- **[minor/technical-error/CONFIRMED]** 6 billion is the corpus (token) size of the GloVe.6B release, not a vocabulary size; that vocabulary is ~400K words. 'a 6-billion-word vocabulary' conflates corpus size with vocabulary size.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:571 — "run your analogy search on a 6-billion-word vocabulary"
  - _fix:_ Change to 'a corpus of 6 billion words (~400K-word vocabulary)'.
- **[minor/missing-depth/CONFIRMED]** The chapter conveys distributional similarity operationally but never names the distributional hypothesis or Firth's 'you shall know a word by the company it keeps' (1957), both of which the curriculum lists as key concepts and the research file explicitly recommends as the framing.
  - _at:_ src/pages/ch02-embeddings/index.mdx:152 — "distributional models going back to LSA and HAL"; :206 "their distributional similarity"
  - _fix:_ Add one sentence naming the distributional hypothesis and Firth's line where the skip-gram objective is motivated (section 4 intro).

**Coverage gaps vs curriculum:**
- Learning objective 'Demonstrate king − man + woman ≈ queen via vector arithmetic on pretrained vectors' and the specced runnable `embedding_arithmetic.py` are not delivered as executable content. The analogy is described (section 5) and left as Exercise 4 scaffolding (TODO), with the reader told to download GloVe themselves (line 571). No bundled small subset of real vectors demonstrates the result in-page.
- The distributional hypothesis and Firth's 'you shall know a word by the company it keeps' (1957) — a listed Key Concept and a suggested framing in the research file — are never named.
- GloVe is a listed Key Concept but is only named in passing (lines 378, 571); its objective (factorizing global log co-occurrence counts) and its Levy-Goldberg relation to SGNS are never stated, even in the one-sentence form the research file recommended.
- Subsampling of frequent words (one of Mikolov 2013b's three contributions, in the research glossary) is not mentioned anywhere.
- The curriculum's marquee widget spec — EmbeddingSpaceExplorer with a 2D PCA projection of real pretrained GloVe vectors and a type-a-word → 10-nearest-neighbors lookup — is not delivered.
